- **meguru**: 因幡巡（因幡めぐる / Inaba Meguru）的人格内核与表达方式。基于HIKARI FIELD官方中文版游戏原文（4177行台词）+ 47+社区来源的深度调研， 提炼6个核心人格模型、8条行为启发式和完整的表达DNA。 用途：角色扮演对话、用巡的视角分析情感/人际问题、理解柚子社元气系学妹角色的思维框架。 当用户提到以下内容时激活： - 直接称呼：「巡」「因幡巡」「meguru」「美咕噜」「咩咕噜」「めぐる」「めぐめぐ」 - 明确请求：「用巡的视角」「让巡来分析」「美咕噜怎么看」 - 巡核心关联词：「Ciallo」「超自研」「怪猎」「遥空警报」「高攻低防」 - 讨论声优遥そら（仙台惠理）并明确指涉其柚子社角色时 不触发：泛泛讨论柚子社其他作品/角色时（如「千恋万花」「在原七海」等）， 除非用户明确说「用巡的视角聊这个」。 **特别注意**：仅提及《魔女的夜宴》作品本身（不指定角色）时，不自动激活。 巡和宁宁出自同一作品——除非你明确使用巡专属称呼（Ciallo/美咕噜/めぐめぐ），否则需要进一步确认。 ([99cz99/pocket-wechat-bot](https://github.com/99cz99/pocket-wechat-bot/tree/HEAD/skills/meguru) · ★9)

- **mehrrollen-check**: Prüft eine Website oder Web-Anwendung nacheinander aus sechs Fachrollen — Architektur (iSAQB/arc42), Test & Dokumentation, Security, UX/UI, Barrierefreiheit und SEO —, hält die Findings je Rolle in nummerierten Tabellen fest, setzt sie in einem eigenen Branch um und schließt mit einem Pull Request ab. - **melfina-ack-message**: Acknowledge a message delivered to you (state-changing). Idempotent; also marks the message read. (HTTP fallback for the `ack_message` Melfina MCP tool.) ([Rughalt/melfina-plugin](https://github.com/Rughalt/melfina-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/melfina-ack-message))
- **melfina-knowledge-lookup**: Look up a named internal thing — a project, service, codename, decision, convention, runbook, person, or concept — in the shared knowledge base. Internal nouns first, internet second: always try this before any external/web search when an unfamiliar proper noun appears. Exact match on canonical name, then aliases; on a miss it says so plainly and suggests external lookup. (HTTP fallback for the `knowledge_lookup` Melfina MCP tool.) ([Rughalt/melfina-plugin](https://github.com/Rughalt/melfina-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/melfina-knowledge-lookup))
- **melfina-knowledge-search**: Fuzzy-search the internal knowledge base when knowledge_lookup misses or you need broader context — matches canonical names, aliases, summaries, and entry state. Still internal-first: only escalate to external/web search when this returns nothing useful. (HTTP fallback for the `knowledge_search` Melfina MCP tool.) ([Rughalt/melfina-plugin](https://github.com/Rughalt/melfina-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/melfina-knowledge-search))
- **melfina-knowledge-upsert**: Create or update a canonical knowledge entry — a project, service, decision, convention, runbook, person, or concept the install should know by name. - **melfina-list-agents**: List the other agents on this install — name plus their short public description, so you know who shares your spaces. - **melfina-list-files**: List every path in your private file tree. (HTTP fallback for the `list_files` Melfina MCP tool.) ([Rughalt/melfina-plugin](https://github.com/Rughalt/melfina-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/melfina-list-files))
- **melfina-list-inbox**: List messages delivered to you, newest first. Returns metadata and a short body preview — call read_message for the full body and attachments. - **melfina-list-message-recipients**: List the agents you can send a message to — id and display name. Address messages by id: names are display-only. - **melfina-list-sent-messages**: List messages you have sent, newest first. Returns metadata and a short body preview — call read_message for the full body and attachments. - **melfina-list-shared**: List shared spaces you can read — or, given a space name, the paths inside it. (HTTP fallback for the `list_shared` Melfina MCP tool.) ([Rughalt/melfina-plugin](https://github.com/Rughalt/melfina-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/melfina-list-shared))
- **melfina-read-file**: Read a file from your private file tree. Text comes back verbatim; images come back as MCP image content; other binaries need `format=base64` or a raw GET /api/files/{path}. - **melfina-read-file-binary**: Read a binary file from your private file tree. Images come back as MCP image content (viewable natively); other binaries return a JSON payload with path, mime, and content_base64. For large downloads prefer `GET /api/files/{path}` — raw bytes, no base64. Text files belong on `read_file`. (HTTP fallback for the `read_file_binary` Melfina MCP tool.) ([Rughalt/melfina-plugin](https://github.com/Rughalt/melfina-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/melfina-read-file-binary))

- **melfina-read-message**: Read one message you sent or received — full body plus attachment snapshots. Reading as the recipient marks it read (state-changing for inbox items). - **melfina-read-persona**: Read your persona and memories — your self-authored identity and the memory log you keep across conversations. (HTTP fallback for the `read_persona` Melfina MCP tool.) - **melfina-read-shared**: Read a file from a shared space you have a grant on. - **melfina-send-message**: Send a directed message to one agent (delivers to their inbox; state-changing). - **melfina-write-file**: Write a file to your private file tree (overwrites existing content). Pass `content` for text; pass `content_base64` (+ optional `mime`) for binary — UTF-8 text stays a text file either way. For large binaries prefer PUT /api/files/{path}, which takes raw bytes without base64. (HTTP fallback for the `write_file` Melfina MCP tool.) ([Rughalt/melfina-plugin](https://github.com/Rughalt/melfina-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/melfina-write-file))
- **melfina-write-file-binary**: Write a binary file (image, PDF, any non-text bytes) to your private file tree. Send the raw bytes in `content_base64` (base64-encoded); the stored mime is always sniffed from the actual bytes, so a wrong `mime` claim cannot stick. UTF-8 text written this way still lands as a normal text file. For payloads over ~1 MB prefer `PUT /api/files/{path}` — raw bytes, no base64 overhead. (HTTP fallback for the `write_file_binary` Melfina MCP tool.) ([Rughalt/melfina-plugin](https://github.com/Rughalt/melfina-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/melfina-write-file-binary))
- **melfina-write-shared**: Write a file into a shared space you have a write grant on (overwrites existing content). Pass `content` for text; pass `content_base64` (+ optional `mime`) for binary — UTF-8 text stays a text file either way. For large binaries prefer PUT /api/shared/{space}/files/{path}, which takes raw bytes without base64. (HTTP fallback for the `write_shared` Melfina MCP tool.) ([Rughalt/melfina-plugin](https://github.com/Rughalt/melfina-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/melfina-write-shared))

- **mellea-logging**: Best-practices guide for adding or reviewing logging in the Mellea codebase. Covers when to use log_context() vs a dedicated logger call, canonical field names, reserved attribute constraints, async/thread safety, and what events deserve dedicated log lines. Use when: adding a new log call; reviewing a PR that touches MelleaLogger; deciding where to inject context fields; debugging why a field is missing from a log record; or ensuring consistency with the project logging conventions. ([generative-computing/mellea](https://github.com/generative-computing/mellea/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/mellea-logging) · ★1.9k)
- **mellions-bug-audit**: Load this when asked to audit a subsystem, hunt for bugs, investigate a suspected defect, verify whether a reported problem is real, or establish root cause and blast radius before any issue or fix exists. Triggers — "audit this", "find bugs in", "is this actually broken", "root cause this", "what is the blast radius", "verify this finding", "review this subsystem for defects". Produces findings, never code and never issues. Not for planning or writing the fix (mellions-issue-remediation) or reviewing a diff that already exists. ([LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen](https://github.com/LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen/tree/HEAD/skills/mellions-bug-audit) · ★1)
- **mellions-comment-hygiene**: Load this whenever writing, modifying, reviewing, auditing or refactoring code, and whenever a comment is being relied on to establish what code does — a comment is an untrusted claim, never evidence of behaviour, completion or safety. Triggers — "clean up the comments", "comment hygiene", "are these comments accurate", "the comment says X", "remove stale comments", "should this comment stay", "document this function", "why is this commented out", and any review or audit where a comment is offered as proof that code is correct, complete or removed. Governs in-code comments only: not user-faci… ([LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen](https://github.com/LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen/tree/HEAD/skills/mellions-comment-hygiene) · ★1)
- **mellions-continuity**: Load this at the start of any session that did not attend the one before it — a crash, a compaction, a restart, a switch between Claude and Codex, coming back tomorrow — whenever you find open work you have no memory of, and before acting on anything an earlier session wrote down. Triggers — "where were we", "what was I doing", "continue where you left off", "the session died", "pick this back up", "did that merge go through", "resume the assignment", "I lost the context". Not for choosing new work (survey), writing up what happened (report), or deciding what a run should teach the next one (… ([LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen](https://github.com/LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen/tree/HEAD/skills/mellions-continuity) · ★1)
- **mellions-deep-research**: Load this before deciding anything the evidence at hand cannot settle — a work item's premise, a root cause, a remedy's mechanism, another session's or a subagent's claim, a provider's behaviour, what production actually does, which document has authority — and before publishing a claim about a file, run or record you have not opened. Triggers — "is this still true", "what does the code actually do", "the issue says", "the doc says", "the session says", "find out", "investigate", "establish", "where is the evidence". Not for deciding what to do with what you found (mellions-reasoning), provin… ([LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen](https://github.com/LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen/tree/HEAD/skills/mellions-deep-research) · ★1)
- **mellions-delegation**: How to run a repository work session, and how to review the work of one you did not open — what a dispatch contains, how to judge what comes back, how to review a peer's pull request and whether merging it is yours, when to isolate work in the disposable sandbox, and when to close. Use when opening a session, writing a dispatch, deciding whether a session's report is trustworthy, unblocking one that is stuck, deciding a session is finished, or when a survey shows a peer's draft pull request waiting for a review nobody has given. Triggers — "delegate this", "open a session", "spin up a worker"… ([LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen](https://github.com/LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen/tree/HEAD/skills/mellions-delegation) · ★1)
- **mellions-environment**: What this host can actually do, and how to establish it rather than assume it — toolchains, git and GitHub access, container runtimes, the disposable sandbox, and where the repositories are. Use when deciding whether a task is possible here, choosing what to ask a session to run, telling one where a repository lives, or answering what tooling is available. Triggers — "can we run", "is X installed", "where is the repo", "what version of", "run the tests", "use the sandbox", "pnpm is not on PATH". ([LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen](https://github.com/LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen/tree/HEAD/skills/mellions-environment) · ★1)
- **mellions-falsification**: Load this before a test, a mutation, a revert arm or a green run is cited as proof that a fix holds — and again before writing "falsified" in a pull request. Triggers — "prove it holds", "falsify", "neutralise the fix", "watch it fail", "the test passes", "is the test real", "mutation", "revert arm", "the suite is green", "proof section". Not for deciding what to do with the result (mellions-reasoning) or for establishing what is true in the first place (mellions-deep-research). ([LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen](https://github.com/LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen/tree/HEAD/skills/mellions-falsification) · ★1)
- **mellions-harness-rule**: Turn a lesson into a check the repository enforces itself. Use when a defect has just been fixed and the same mistake could be made again — especially when it exists or existed in sibling repositories, or when the reason it was wrong is invisible from the call site. ([LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen](https://github.com/LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen/tree/HEAD/skills/mellions-harness-rule) · ★1)
- **mellions-issue-closure**: Load this when work on an issue is finished and the question is whether the issue may close — after a merge, after a deploy, when an issue's defect turns out to be already gone at HEAD, or when a survey shows resolved work still open. It decides which event the issue actually closes on, what proof must be on the record first, and whether closing is yours or the owner's. Triggers — "close issue NN", "can we close this", "is this done", "post the proof", "the PR merged, close it", "this was fixed elsewhere". Not for deciding whether a defect is real (mellions-bug-audit) or for the implementatio… ([LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen](https://github.com/LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen/tree/HEAD/skills/mellions-issue-closure) · ★1)
- **mellions-issue-creation**: Load this when a confirmed finding, an audit result or a piece of work is about to become an issue on the tracker — before filing anything, to check the finding is established at the base branch now, that no issue, pull request or claim already covers it, and to write the work contract in the taxonomy this Skill's assets carry. Triggers — "open an issue for", "file this", "turn the finding into an issue", "create a GitHub issue", "draft the issue", "should this be an issue". Not for deciding whether a defect is real (mellions-bug-audit) or for planning the fix (mellions-issue-resolution-propo… ([LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen](https://github.com/LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen/tree/HEAD/skills/mellions-issue-creation) · ★1)
- **mellions-issue-remediation**: Load this whenever the instruction is to remediate, fix, build, ship or land the work a defect or work item describes — before the worktree is cut. Triggers — "remediate this", "fix issue NN", "implement the fix", "ship the remediation", "open the PR for this", "land this work", "start the implementation". Not for deciding whether a defect is real (mellions-bug-audit) or for closing the issue afterwards (mellions-issue-closure). ([LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen](https://github.com/LetA-Tech/mellions-coxen/tree/HEAD/skills/mellions-issue-remediation) · ★1)
